(2.61)

Note that for simplicity we have omitted to specify the colour structure.
The three terms on the r.h.s. of Eq. (
2.61
) generate different topologies, usually referred to, respectively, as Weak Exchange (WE), Pauli Interference (PI) and Weak Annihilation (WA)
10
10

10

It is worth mentioning that for the description of baryons, the WE and PI topologies are interchanged.
, schematically shown in Figure
2.3
. In analogy to Eq. (
2.10
) we then introduce the notation

{\cal T}^{(4q)}={\cal T}^{\rm WE}+{\cal T}^{\rm PI}+{\cal T}^{\rm WA}\,,

(2.62)

where the superscript
(4q)
indicates that four-quark fields are not contracted and

{\cal T}^{X}=\Bigg(C_{1}^{2}\,{\cal T}_{11}^{X}+2\,C_{1}\,C_{2}\,{\cal T}^{X}_{12}+C_{2}^{2}\,{\cal T}_{22}^{X}\Bigg)\,,

(2.63)

with
X
labelling the specific topology. We emphasise that the three contributions in Eq. (
2.63
) correspond to the same Dirac structure and differ only by the contraction of the colour indices, namely, starting with the case of Weak Exchange, we can conveniently define the following tensor in colour space

\displaystyle{\cal A}^{ijmn}_{klrs}

\displaystyle=\frac{G_{F}^{2}}{2}|V_{q_{1}b}|^{2}|V_{q_{3}q_{2}}|^{2}\ i\int d^{4}x\,\Bigl(\bar{b}^{i}\,\gamma_{\nu}(1-\gamma_{5})\,iS^{(q_{1})}_{kl}(0,x)\gamma_{\mu}(1-\gamma_{5})b^{j}(x)

\displaystyle\times\,\bar{q}_{3}^{m}(x)\,\gamma^{\mu}(1-\gamma_{5})\,iS^{(q_{2})}_{rs}(x,0)\,\gamma^{\nu}(1-\gamma_{5})\,q_{3}^{n}\Bigr)+(x\leftrightarrow 0)\,,

(2.64)

so that the three terms in Eq. (
2.63
) are respectively given by

\displaystyle{\cal T}_{11}^{\rm WE}={\cal A}^{ijmn}_{klrs}\,\delta^{i}_{k}\delta^{j}_{l}\delta^{m}_{r}\delta^{n}_{s}\,,\quad{\cal T}_{12}^{\rm WE}={\cal A}^{ijmn}_{klrs}\,\delta^{i}_{s}\delta^{j}_{l}\delta^{m}_{r}\delta^{n}_{k}\,,\quad{\cal T}_{22}^{\rm WE}={\cal A}^{ijmn}_{klrs}\,\delta^{in}\delta^{jm}\delta_{ks}\delta_{lr}\,.

(2.65)

Similarly, for the Pauli Interference topology, we introduce the tensor

\displaystyle{\cal B}^{ijmn}_{klrs}

\displaystyle=\frac{G_{F}^{2}}{2}|V_{q_{1}b}|^{2}|V_{q_{3}q_{2}}|^{2}\ i\int d^{4}x\,\Bigl(\bar{b}^{i}\,\gamma_{\nu}(1-\gamma_{5})\,iS^{(q_{1})}_{kl}(0,x)\gamma_{\mu}(1-\gamma_{5})b^{j}(x)

\displaystyle\times\,\bar{q}_{2}^{m}\,\gamma^{\nu}(1-\gamma_{5})\,iS^{(q_{3})}_{rs}(0,x)\,\gamma^{\mu}(1-\gamma_{5})\,q_{2}^{n}(x)\Bigr)+(x\leftrightarrow 0)\,,

(2.66)

and the three contributions in Eq. (
2.63
) read

\displaystyle{\cal T}_{11}^{\rm PI}={\cal B}^{ijmn}_{klrs}\,\delta^{i}_{k}\delta^{j}_{l}\delta^{m}_{r}\delta^{n}_{s}\,,\quad{\cal T}_{12}^{\rm PI}={\cal B}^{ijmn}_{klrs}\,\delta^{i}_{k}\delta^{j}_{r}\delta^{m}_{l}\delta^{n}_{s}\,,\quad{\cal T}_{22}^{\rm PI}={\cal T}_{11}^{\rm PI}\,.

(2.67)

Finally, in the case of Weak Annihilation, we define

\displaystyle{\cal C}^{ijmn}_{klrs}

\displaystyle=-\frac{G_{F}^{2}}{2}|V_{q_{1}b}|^{2}|V_{q_{3}q_{2}}|^{2}\ \,i\int d^{4}x\,\Bigl(\bar{b}^{i}\,\gamma_{\nu}(1-\gamma_{5})\,q^{j}_{1}\,\bar{q}^{m}_{1}(x)\gamma_{\mu}(1-\gamma_{5})b^{n}(x)\Bigr)

\displaystyle\times\,{\rm Tr}\Big[\gamma^{\nu}(1-\gamma_{5})\,iS^{(q_{2})}_{kl}(0,x)\,\gamma^{\mu}(1-\gamma_{5})\,iS^{(q_{3})}_{rs}(x,0)\Big]+(x\leftrightarrow 0)\,,

(2.68)

where the minus sign and the trace over spinor indices follow from the fermion-loop and

\displaystyle{\cal T}_{11}^{\rm WA}={\cal C}^{ijmn}_{klrs}\,\delta^{ij}\delta^{mn}\delta_{ks}\delta_{lr}\,,\quad{\cal T}_{12}^{\rm WA}={\cal C}^{ijmn}_{klrs}\,\delta^{i}_{k}\delta^{j}_{s}\delta_{lr}\delta^{mn}\,,\quad{\cal T}_{22}^{\rm WA}={\cal C}^{ijmn}_{klrs}\,\delta^{i}_{k}\delta^{j}_{s}\delta^{m}_{r}\delta^{n}_{l}\,.

(2.69)

2.2.1 Computation of Im
{\cal T}^{(4q)}_{6}

To compute the leading power corrections to the WE, PI and WA topologies, namely the dimension-six contribution to the four-quark operators, we can ignore the QCD interaction with the background field and set in Eqs. (
2.64
), (
2.66
), (
2.68
)

S^{(q_{i})}_{jk}(x,y)=S^{(q_{i})}_{0}(x-y)\,\delta_{jk}\,,\qquad i=1,2,3\,,

(2.70)

where
S_{0}^{(q_{i})}(x-y)
is the free-quark propagator associated to
q_{i}
, and note that we consider now the general case of
b\to q_{1}\bar{q}_{2}q_{3}
transition.
Higher order terms in Eq. (
2.70
), cf. Eqs. (
1.76
), (
1.121
), describe soft gluon corrections to the four-quark operators and lead to contributions to the HQE of at least dimension-eight. These will not be discussed in the present work. However now, the loop-computation is more easily performed in momentum space and we will use, contrary to what we have done in the previous section, the Fourier representation of the propagator in Eq. (
2.70
).
Substituting Eq. (
1.73
) into the first of the three expressions in Eq. (
2.65
) yields
11
11

11

Note that we take into account the replacement in Eq. (
2.26
), supplemented with the corresponding one for the light spectator antiquark.

\displaystyle{\cal T}_{11}^{\rm WE}

\displaystyle=-\,G_{F}^{2}|V_{q_{1}b}|^{2}|V_{q_{3}q_{2}}|^{2}\,i\int d^{4}x\,\int\frac{d^{4}l}{(2\pi)^{4}}\,\int\frac{d^{4}k}{(2\pi)^{4}}e^{-i(p+k-l)\cdot x}\,\Bigl(\bar{b}^{i}\gamma_{\nu}(1-\gamma_{5})

\displaystyle\times\left(\frac{\not{l}}{l^{2}-m_{1}^{2}+i\varepsilon}\right)\gamma_{\mu}(1-\gamma_{5})b^{i}\bar{q}_{3}^{j}\gamma^{\mu}(1-\gamma_{5})\left(\frac{\not{k}}{k^{2}-m_{2}^{2}+i\varepsilon}\right)\gamma^{\nu}(1-\gamma_{5})q_{3}^{j}\Bigr)\,,

(2.71)

where
p^{\mu}=p^{\mu}_{b}+p^{\mu}_{q_{3}}
.
In deriving Eq. (
2.71
) we have used that
b(x)
and
\bar{q}_{3}(x)
describe respectively, an incoming quark with momentum
p^{\mu}_{b}
and an incoming antiquark with momentum
p^{\mu}_{q_{3}}

12
12

12

For baryons, being both
b
and
q_{3}
quarks, it would be
p^{\mu}=p^{\mu}_{b}-p^{\mu}_{q_{3}}
.
. We notice that a symmetry factor of
2
due to translation invariance has been already included and that the chiral structure of Eq. (
2.71
) implies that all terms in the propagator proportional to an even number of gamma matrices must vanish. Performing the integration over the variables
x^{\mu}
and
k^{\mu}
, leads to

\displaystyle{\cal T}_{11}^{\rm WE}

\displaystyle=-4\,G_{F}^{2}|V_{q_{1}b}|^{2}|V_{q_{3}q_{2}}|^{2}\,i\,\int\frac{d^{4}l}{(2\pi)^{4}}\,\frac{\big(l^{\rho}l^{\sigma}-p^{\rho}l^{\sigma}\big)}{(l^{2}-m_{1}^{2}+i\varepsilon)((l-p)^{2}-m_{2}^{2}+i\varepsilon)}

\displaystyle\times\,g_{\sigma\xi}\,\Big(\bar{b}^{i}\gamma_{\nu}(1-\gamma_{5})\gamma_{\rho}\gamma_{\mu}b^{i}\Big)\Big(\bar{q}_{3}^{j}\gamma^{\mu}\gamma^{\xi}\gamma^{\nu}(1-\gamma_{5})q_{3}^{j}\Big)\,,

(2.72)

where the structure of the four-quark operator in Eq. (
2.72
) can be simplified taking into account the tensor decomposition of three gamma matrices in Eq. (
7
), and the Fierz identity in Eq. (
2.15
), i.e.

\displaystyle g_{\sigma\xi}\Big(\bar{b}^{i}\gamma_{\nu}(1-\gamma_{5})\gamma_{\rho}\gamma_{\mu}b^{i}\Big)\Big(\bar{q}_{3}^{j}\gamma^{\mu}\gamma^{\xi}\gamma^{\nu}(1-\gamma_{5})q_{3}^{j}\Big)

\displaystyle=-2\,\Big(\bar{b}^{i}

\displaystyle\gamma^{\nu}(1-\gamma_{5})q_{3}^{j}\Big)\Big(\bar{q}_{3}^{j}(g_{\sigma\nu}\gamma_{\rho}+g_{\nu\rho}\gamma_{\sigma}-g_{\rho\sigma}\gamma_{\nu})(1-\gamma_{5})b^{i}\Big)\,,

(2.73)

note that we have dropped the contribution of the Levi-Civita tensor since the integral in Eq. (
2.74
) is a symmetric function in the indices
\{\rho,\sigma\}
.
Using the Passarino-Veltman reduction algorithm
[
155
]
, the one-loop tensor integral in Eq. (
2.72
) can be decomposed in terms of one- and two-point one-loop scalar integrals. Note that the latter are ultraviolet (UV) divergent and must be regularised. Performing the computation in dimensional regularisation
[
47
,
48
,
49
,
50
]
with
D=4-2\epsilon
space-time dimensions, one obtains the results listed in Appendix
B
, in which the singularity appears as a single pole in
\epsilon
, see Eqs. (
B.4
), (
B.5
). However, being interested only in the imaginary part of
{\cal T}_{11}^{\rm WE}
which, cf. Eqs. (
B.8
), (
B.9
), is finite for
\epsilon\to 0
, we can use the expressions in Eqs. (
B.10
)-(
B.20
) setting
D=4
. This gives

\displaystyle{\rm Im}\,\Bigg(i\,

\displaystyle\int\frac{d^{4}l}{(2\pi)^{4}}\,\frac{\big(l^{\rho}l^{\sigma}-p^{\rho}l^{\sigma}\big)}{(l^{2}-m_{1}^{2}+i\varepsilon)((l-p)^{2}-m_{2}^{2}+i\varepsilon)}\Bigg)=

\displaystyle\frac{p^{2}\sqrt{\lambda(1,r_{1},r_{2})}}{192\pi}

\displaystyle\Bigg[g^{\rho\sigma}\lambda(1,r_{1},r_{2})-2\frac{p^{\rho}p^{\sigma}}{p^{2}}\Big(2(r_{1}-r_{2})^{2}-(1+r_{1}+r_{2})\Big)\Bigg]\,,

(2.74)

where
r_{i}=m_{i}^{2}/p^{2}

and
\lambda(a,b,c)\equiv(a-b-c)^{2}-4bc
is the Källen function. Notice that Eq. (
2.74
) is a symmetric function under the exchange
m_{1}\leftrightarrow m_{2}
. Substituting Eqs. (
2.73
), (
2.74
) into Eq. (
2.72
), we then obtain

\displaystyle{\rm Im}\,{\cal T}_{11}^{\rm WE}

\displaystyle=\frac{G_{F}^{2}}{12\pi}|V_{q_{1}b}|^{2}|V_{q_{3}q_{2}}|^{2}\,p^{2}\sqrt{\lambda(1,r_{1},r_{2})}\,

\displaystyle\times\Bigg[\Big((r_{1}-r_{2})^{2}+r_{1}+r_{2}-2\Big)\Big(\bar{b}^{i}\Gamma_{\mu}q_{3}^{j}\Big)\Big(\bar{q}_{3}^{j}\Gamma^{\mu}b^{i}\Big)

\displaystyle-2\,\frac{p^{\mu}p^{\nu}}{p^{2}}\Big(2(r_{1}-r_{2})^{2}-(1+r_{1}+r_{2})\Big)\Big(\bar{b}^{i}\Gamma_{\mu}q_{3}^{j}\Big)\,\Big(\bar{q}_{3}^{j}\Gamma_{\nu}b^{i}\Big)\Bigg]\,.

(2.75)

Finally, we can express the colour-rearranged operators in Eq. (
2.75
) in terms of colour-singlet and colour-octet ones using Eq. (
2.9
), this yields

\displaystyle{\rm Im}\,{\cal T}_{11}^{\rm WE}

\displaystyle=\frac{G_{F}^{2}}{12\pi}|V_{q_{1}b}|^{2}|V_{q_{3}q_{2}}|^{2}\,p^{2}\sqrt{\lambda(1,r_{1},r_{2})}\,\Bigg\{\Big((r_{1}-r_{2})^{2}+r_{1}+r_{2}-2\Big)

\displaystyle\times\,\Bigg[\frac{1}{N_{c}}\Big(\bar{b}^{i}\Gamma_{\nu}q_{3}^{i}\Big)\Big(\bar{q}_{3}^{j}\Gamma^{\nu}b^{j}\Big)+2\,\Big(\bar{b}^{i}\Gamma_{\nu}t^{a}_{ij}q_{3}^{j}\Big)\Big(\bar{q}_{3}^{l}\Gamma^{\nu}t^{a}_{lm}b^{m}\Big)\Bigg]

\displaystyle-\,2\frac{p^{\mu}p^{\nu}}{p^{2}}\Big(2(r_{1}-r_{2})^{2}-(1+r_{1}+r_{2})\Big)\,\Bigg[\frac{1}{N_{c}}\Big(\bar{b}^{i}\Gamma_{\mu}q_{3}^{i}\Big)\Big(\bar{q}_{3}^{j}\Gamma_{\nu}b^{j}\Big)

\displaystyle+2\Big(\bar{b}^{i}\,\Gamma_{\mu}\,q_{3}^{i}\Big)\,\Big(\bar{q}_{3}^{j}\,\Gamma_{\nu}\,b^{j}\Big)\Bigg]\Bigg\}\,.

(2.76)

To compute the remaining colour structures we substitute Eq. (
1.73
) into the second and third term of Eq. (
2.65
), obtaining respectively

\displaystyle{\cal T}_{12}^{\rm WE}

\displaystyle=-\,G_{F}^{2}|V_{q_{1}b}|^{2}|V_{q_{3}q_{2}}|^{2}\,i\int d^{4}x\,\int\frac{d^{4}l}{(2\pi)^{4}}\,\int\frac{d^{4}k}{(2\pi)^{4}}e^{-i(p+k-l)\cdot x}\,\Biggl[\bar{b}^{i}\gamma_{\nu}(1-\gamma_{5})

\displaystyle\times\left(\frac{\not{l}}{l^{2}-m_{1}^{2}+i\varepsilon}\right)\gamma_{\mu}(1-\gamma_{5})b^{j}\bar{q}_{3}^{j}\gamma^{\mu}(1-\gamma_{5})\left(\frac{\not{k}}{k^{2}-m_{2}^{2}+i\varepsilon}\right)\gamma^{\nu}(1-\gamma_{5})q_{3}^{i}\Biggr]\,,

(2.77)

and

{\cal T}_{22}^{\rm WE}=N_{c}\,{\cal T}_{12}^{\rm WE}\,,

(2.78)

with the colour factor in Eq. (
2.78
) following from
\delta_{ii}=N_{c}
.
The calculation of Eq. (
2.77
) proceeds in the very same way as for Eq. (
2.71
). For brevity we only state here the final expression which reads

\displaystyle{\rm Im}\,{\cal T}_{12}^{\rm WE}

\displaystyle=\frac{G_{F}^{2}}{12\pi}|V_{q_{1}b}|^{2}|V_{q_{3}q_{2}}|^{2}\,p^{2}\sqrt{\lambda(1,r_{1},r_{2})}\,\Bigg[\Big((r_{1}-r_{2})^{2}+r_{1}+r_{2}-2\Big)

\displaystyle\times\Big(\bar{b}^{i}\Gamma_{\nu}q_{3}^{i}\Big)\Big(\bar{q}_{3}^{j}\Gamma^{\nu}b^{j}\Big)-2\frac{p^{\mu}p^{\nu}}{p^{2}}\Big(2(r_{1}-r_{2})^{2}-(1+r_{1}+r_{2})\Big)\Big(\bar{b}^{i}\Gamma_{\mu}q_{3}^{i}\Big)\Big(\bar{q}_{3}^{j}\Gamma_{\nu}b^{j}\Big)\Bigg]\,,

(2.79)

note that in this case, the result contains already colour-singlet and colour-rearranged operators and we do not need to further use Eq. (
2.9
).
Substituting Eqs. (
2.76
), (
2.79
), and (
2.78
), into Eq. (
2.63
), we readily arrive at

\displaystyle{\rm Im}\,{\cal T}^{\rm WE}

\displaystyle=\frac{G_{F}^{2}}{12\pi}|V_{q_{1}b}|^{2}|V_{q_{3}q_{2}}|^{2}\,p^{2}\sqrt{\lambda(1,r_{1},r_{2})}\,\Bigg\{k_{1}\Bigg[\,\omega_{1}(r_{1},r_{2})\,\Big(\bar{b}^{i}\Gamma_{\mu}q_{3}^{i}\Big)\Big(\bar{q}_{3}^{j}\Gamma^{\mu}b^{j}\Big)

\displaystyle-2\frac{p^{\mu}p^{\nu}}{p^{2}}\omega_{2}(r_{1},r_{2})\Big(\bar{b}^{i}\Gamma_{\mu}q_{3}^{i}\Big)\Big(\bar{q}_{3}^{j}\Gamma_{\nu}b^{j}\Big)\Bigg]+k_{2}\Bigg[\omega_{1}(r_{1},r_{2})\Big(\bar{b}^{i}\Gamma_{\mu}t^{a}_{ij}q_{3}^{j}\Big)\Big(\bar{q}_{3}^{l}\Gamma^{\mu}t^{a}_{lm}b^{m}\Big)

\displaystyle-2\frac{p^{\mu}p^{\nu}}{p^{2}}\omega_{2}(r_{1},r_{2})\Big(\bar{b}^{i}\Gamma_{\mu}t^{a}_{ij}q_{3}^{j}\Big)\Big(\bar{q}_{3}^{l}\Gamma_{\nu}t^{a}_{lm}b^{m}\Big)\Bigg]\Bigg\}+\ldots\,,

(2.80)

where the ellipsis refer to power suppressed contributions arising from corrections to the propagator of order
{\cal O}(1/m_{b}^{2})
and for the sake of a more compact notation we have introduced the following combinations of Wilson coefficients

\displaystyle k_{1}=\frac{1}{N_{c}}\,C_{1}^{2}+2\,C_{1}C_{2}+N_{c}\,C_{2}^{2}\,,\qquad k_{2}=2\,C_{1}^{2}\,,

(2.81)

while

\displaystyle\omega_{1}(a,b)=(a-b)^{2}+a+b-2\,,\qquad\omega_{2}(a,b)=2\,(a-b)^{2}-(1+a+b)\,.

(2.82)

We now turn to consider the contribution of Pauli Interference. Inserting Eq. (
1.73
) into the first expression in Eq. (
2.67
), gives

\displaystyle{\cal T}_{11}^{\rm PI}

\displaystyle=-\,G_{F}^{2}|V_{q_{1}b}|^{2}|V_{q_{3}q_{2}}|^{2}\,i\int d^{4}x\,\int\frac{d^{4}l}{(2\pi)^{4}}\,\int\frac{d^{4}k}{(2\pi)^{4}}e^{-i(p-k-l)\cdot x}\,\Biggl(\bar{b}^{i}\gamma_{\nu}(1-\gamma_{5})

\displaystyle\times\left(\frac{\not{k}}{k^{2}-m_{1}^{2}+i\varepsilon}\right)\gamma_{\mu}(1-\gamma_{5})b^{i}\bar{q}_{2}^{j}\gamma^{\nu}(1-\gamma_{5})\left(\frac{\not{l}}{l^{2}-m_{3}^{2}+i\varepsilon}\right)\gamma^{\mu}(1-\gamma_{5})q_{2}^{j}\Biggr)\,,

(2.83)

here
p^{\mu}=p_{b}^{\mu}-p_{q_{2}}^{\mu}
, which follows from the fact that
q_{2}(x)
describes an outgoing antiquark with momentum
p^{\mu}_{q_{2}}

13
13

13

For baryons, being both
b
and
q_{2}
quarks, it would be
p^{\mu}=p^{\mu}_{b}+p^{\mu}_{q_{2}}
.
.
The integration over the variables
x^{\mu}
and
k^{\mu}
yields

\displaystyle{\cal T}_{11}^{\rm PI}

\displaystyle=4\,G_{F}^{2}|V_{q_{1}b}|^{2}|V_{q_{3}q_{2}}|^{2}\,i\,\int\frac{d^{4}l}{(2\pi)^{4}}\,\frac{(l^{\rho}l^{\sigma}-p^{\rho}l^{\sigma})}{(l^{2}-m_{3}^{2}+i\varepsilon)((l-p)^{2}-m_{1}^{2}+i\varepsilon)}

\displaystyle\times\,g_{\sigma\xi}\,\Big(\bar{b}^{i}\gamma_{\nu}(1-\gamma_{5})\gamma_{\rho}\gamma_{\mu}b^{i}\Big)\Big(\bar{q}_{3}^{j}\gamma^{\nu}\gamma^{\xi}\gamma^{\mu}(1-\gamma_{5})q_{3}^{j}\Big)\,,

(2.84)

where the four-quark operator can be simplified similarly to Eq. (
2.73
) and using again Eq. (
2.9
),
while the imaginary part of the one-loop integral in Eq. (
2.84
) is obtained, taking into account the symmetry under the exchange
m_{1}\leftrightarrow m_{3}
, by replacing
r_{2}\to r_{3}
into Eq. (
2.74
). A straightforward calculation leads to

\displaystyle{\rm Im}\,{\cal T}_{11}^{\rm PI}

\displaystyle=\frac{G_{F}^{2}}{2\pi}|V_{q_{1}b}|^{2}|V_{q_{3}q_{2}}|^{2}p^{2}\,\sqrt{\lambda(1,r_{1},r_{3})}\,(1-r_{1}-r_{3})

\displaystyle\times\,\Bigg(\frac{1}{N_{c}}\Big(\bar{b}^{i}\Gamma_{\mu}q_{2}^{i}\Big)\Big(\bar{q}_{2}^{j}\Gamma^{\mu}b^{j}\Big)+2\Big(\bar{b}^{i}\Gamma_{\mu}t^{a}_{ij}q_{2}^{j}\Big)\Big(\bar{q}_{2}^{l}\Gamma^{\mu}\,t^{a}_{lm}b^{m}\Big)\Bigg)\,.

(2.85)

The remaining colour structure is obtained by substituting Eq. (
1.73
) into the second term of Eq. (
2.67
), i.e.

\displaystyle{\cal T}_{12}^{\rm PI}

\displaystyle=-\,G_{F}^{2}|V_{q_{1}b}|^{2}|V_{q_{3}q_{2}}|^{2}\,i\int d^{4}x\,\int\frac{d^{4}l}{(2\pi)^{4}}\,\int\frac{d^{4}k}{(2\pi)^{4}}e^{-i(p-k-l)\cdot x}\,\Biggl(\bar{b}^{i}\gamma_{\nu}(1-\gamma_{5})

\displaystyle\times\left(\frac{\not{k}}{k^{2}-m_{1}^{2}+i\varepsilon}\right)\gamma_{\mu}(1-\gamma_{5})b^{j}\bar{q}_{2}^{j}\gamma^{\nu}(1-\gamma_{5})\left(\frac{\not{l}}{l^{2}-m_{3}^{2}+i\varepsilon}\right)\gamma^{\mu}(1-\gamma_{5})q_{2}^{i}\Biggr)\,.

(2.86)

All the steps proceed analogously to the case of
{\cal T}_{11}^{\rm PI}
, the only difference being that the result is already expressed in terms of colour-singlet operators and we do not need to use Eq. (
2.9
). It is in fact easy to show that

\displaystyle{\rm Im}\,{\cal T}_{12}^{\rm PI}

\displaystyle=\frac{G_{F}^{2}}{2\pi}|V_{q_{1}b}|^{2}|V_{q_{3}q_{2}}|^{2}\,p^{2}\,\sqrt{\lambda(1,r_{1},r_{3})}(1-r_{1}-r_{3})\,\Big(\bar{b}^{i}\Gamma_{\mu}q_{2}^{i}\Big)\Big(\bar{q}_{2}^{j}\Gamma^{\mu}b^{j}\Big)\,,

(2.87)

and from Eqs. (
2.85
), (
2.87
), (
2.63
) we readily obtain that

\displaystyle{\rm Im}\,{\cal T}^{\rm PI}

\displaystyle=\frac{G_{F}^{2}}{2\pi}|V_{q_{1}b}|^{2}|V_{q_{3}q_{2}}|^{2}p^{2}\,\sqrt{\lambda(1,r_{1},r_{3})}\,(1-r_{1}-r_{3})

\displaystyle\times\Bigg[k_{3}\Big(\bar{b}^{i}\Gamma_{\mu}q_{2}^{i}\Big)\Big(\bar{q}_{2}^{j}\Gamma^{\mu}b^{j}\Big)+k_{4}\Big(\bar{b}^{i}\Gamma_{\mu}t^{a}_{ij}q_{2}^{j}\Big)\Big(\bar{q}_{2}^{l}\Gamma^{\mu}t^{a}_{lm}b^{m}\Big)\Bigg]+\ldots\,,

(2.88)

with

k_{3}=\frac{1}{N_{c}}\Big(C_{1}^{2}+C_{2}^{2}\Big)+2\,C_{1}C_{2}\,,\qquad k_{4}=2\,\Big(C_{1}^{2}+C_{2}^{2}\Big)\,.

(2.89)

Finally we discuss the Weak Annihilation topology. Substituting Eq. (
1.73
) into the first expression of Eq. (
2.69
) yields

\displaystyle{\cal T}_{11}^{\rm WA}

\displaystyle=N_{c}\,G_{F}^{2}|V_{q_{1}b}|^{2}|V_{q_{3}q_{2}}|^{2}i\int d^{4}x\int\frac{d^{4}l}{(2\pi)^{4}}\int\frac{d^{4}k}{(2\pi)^{4}}e^{-i(p+k-l)\cdot x}\,\Big(\bar{b}^{i}\gamma_{\nu}(1-\gamma_{5})q_{1}^{i}\Big)

\displaystyle\times\Big(\bar{q}_{1}^{j}\gamma_{\mu}(1-\gamma_{5})b^{j}\Big){\rm Tr}\left[\gamma^{\nu}(1-\gamma_{5})\left(\frac{\not{l}}{l^{2}-m_{2}^{2}+i\varepsilon}\right)\gamma^{\mu}(1-\gamma_{5})\left(\frac{\not{k}}{k^{2}-m_{3}^{2}+i\varepsilon}\right)\right]\,,

(2.90)

where
p^{\mu}=p_{b}^{\mu}+p_{q_{1}}^{\mu}
, due to the fact that
\bar{q}_{1}(x)
describes an incoming antiparticle with momentum
p^{\mu}_{q_{1}}

14
14

14

For baryons, being both
b
and
q_{1}
quarks, it would be
p^{\mu}=p^{\mu}_{b}-p^{\mu}_{q_{1}}
.
, and we have taken into account that only terms in the propagator proportional to an odd number of gamma matrices contribute to the trace.

Performing the integration over the variables
x^{\mu}
and
k^{\mu}
and evaluating the trace in Eq. (
2.90
), leads to

\displaystyle{\cal T}_{11}^{\rm WA}

\displaystyle=8N_{c}G_{F}^{2}|V_{q_{1}b}|^{2}|V_{q_{3}q_{2}}|^{2}\,i\,\int\frac{d^{4}l}{(2\pi)^{4}}\,\frac{l_{\rho}l_{\sigma}-p_{\rho}l_{\sigma}}{(l^{2}-m_{2}^{2}+i\varepsilon)((l-p)^{2}-m_{3}^{2}+i\varepsilon)}

\displaystyle\times\Big(\bar{b}^{i}\gamma_{\nu}(1-\gamma_{5})q_{1}^{i}\Big)\Big(\bar{q}_{1}^{j}\gamma_{\mu}(1-\gamma_{5})b^{j}\Big)\Big(g^{\nu\rho}g^{\mu\sigma}+g^{\nu\sigma}g^{\mu\rho}-g^{\nu\mu}g^{\rho\sigma}+i\epsilon^{\nu\rho\sigma\mu}\Big)\,.

(2.91)

Again, since the one-loop integral in Eq. (
2.91
) is symmetric under the exchange
m_{2}\leftrightarrow m_{3}
, its imaginary part can be obtained from Eq. (
2.74
) making the replacement
r_{1}\to r_{3}
, namely

\displaystyle{\rm Im}\,{\cal T}_{11}^{\rm WA}

\displaystyle=\frac{N_{c}}{12\pi}G_{F}^{2}|V_{q_{1}b}|^{2}|V_{q_{3}q_{2}}|^{2}p^{2}\sqrt{\lambda(1,r_{3},r_{2})}\Bigg[\Big((r_{3}-r_{2})^{2}+r_{3}+r_{2}-2\Big)

\displaystyle\times\Big(\bar{b}^{i}\Gamma_{\nu}q_{1}^{i}\Big)\Big(\bar{q}_{1}^{j}\Gamma^{\nu}b^{j}\Big)-2\frac{p^{\mu}p^{\nu}}{p^{2}}\Big(2(r_{3}-r_{2})^{2}-(1+r_{3}+r_{2})\Big)\Big(\bar{b}^{i}\Gamma_{\mu}q_{1}^{i}\Big)\Big(\bar{q}_{1}^{j}\Gamma_{\nu}b^{j}\Big)\Bigg]\,.

(2.92)

Note that Eq. (
2.92
) reproduces
{\rm Im}{\cal T}_{22}^{\rm WE}
in Eq. (
2.78
) with the exchange
q_{1}\leftrightarrow q_{3}
. This result is consequence of the Fierz identity in Eq. (
2.15
), and by taking into account Eq. (
2.16
) it follows that

{\cal T}_{mn}^{\rm WA}={\cal T}_{nm}^{{\rm WE}\,(q_{1}\leftrightarrow q_{3})}\,.

(2.93)

From Eq. (
2.93
), we see that the expression for Im
{\cal T}^{\rm WA}
can be immediately obtained from Eq. (
2.80
) by replacing
C_{1}\leftrightarrow C_{2}
, and
q_{1}\leftrightarrow q_{3}
, namely

\displaystyle{\rm Im}\,{\cal T}^{\rm WA}

\displaystyle=\frac{G_{F}^{2}}{12\pi}|V_{q_{1}b}|^{2}|V_{q_{3}q_{2}}|^{2}\,p^{2}\sqrt{\lambda(1,r_{3},r_{2})}\,\Bigg\{k_{5}\Bigg[\,\omega_{1}(r_{3},r_{2})\,\Big(\bar{b}^{i}\Gamma_{\mu}q_{1}^{i}\Big)\Big(\bar{q}_{1}^{j}\Gamma^{\mu}b^{j}\Big)

\displaystyle-2\frac{p^{\mu}p^{\nu}}{p^{2}}\omega_{2}(r_{3},r_{2})\Big(\bar{b}^{i}\Gamma_{\mu}q_{1}^{i}\Big)\Big(\bar{q}_{1}^{j}\Gamma_{\nu}b^{j}\Big)\Bigg]+k_{6}\Bigg[\omega_{1}(r_{3},r_{2})\Big(\bar{b}^{i}\Gamma_{\mu}t^{a}_{ij}q_{1}^{j}\Big)\Big(\bar{q}_{1}^{l}\Gamma^{\mu}t^{a}_{lm}b^{m}\Big)

\displaystyle-2\frac{p^{\mu}p^{\nu}}{p^{2}}\omega_{2}(r_{3},r_{2})\Big(\bar{b}^{i}\Gamma_{\mu}t^{a}_{ij}q_{1}^{j}\Big)\Big(\bar{q}_{1}^{l}\Gamma_{\nu}t^{a}_{lm}b^{m}\Big)\Bigg]\Bigg\}+\ldots\,,

(2.94)

with

\displaystyle k_{5}=N_{c}\,C_{1}^{2}+2\,C_{1}C_{2}+\frac{1}{N_{c}}\,C_{2}^{2}\,,\qquad k_{6}=2\,C_{2}^{2}\,.

(2.95)

In deriving Eqs. (
2.80
), (
2.88
) and (
2.94
) we have only neglected power corrections due to the expansion of the quark propagator, however, because
p^{\mu}
depends on the residual component of the heavy quark momentum
k^{\mu}
as well as on the soft momentum of the light spectator quark
p^{\mu}_{q_{i}}
, the expressions above contain also the information about the interaction of the heavy quark with the light degrees of freedom. In order to single out the dimension-six result, we set
p^{\mu}=m_{b}v^{\mu}+{\cal O}(1/m_{b})
and introduce the rescaled field
b(x)=\exp(-im_{b}v\cdot x)b_{v}(x)
, see Eq. (
1.145
), satisfying
b_{v}(x)=\not{v}b_{v}(x)+{\cal O}(1/m_{b})
, cf. Eq. (
1.148
). We then obtain that

\frac{p^{\mu}p^{\mu}}{p^{2}}\Big(\bar{b}\gamma_{\mu}(1-\gamma_{5})q_{i}\Big)\Big(\bar{q}_{i}\gamma_{\nu}(1-\gamma_{5})b\Big)=\Big(\bar{b}_{v}(1-\gamma_{5})q_{i}\Big)\Big(\bar{q}_{i}(1+\gamma_{5})b_{v}\Big)+{\cal O}\left(\frac{1}{m_{b}}\right)\,,

(2.96)

where for simplicity we have not specified the colour structure, and that the dimension-six contributions to
{\rm Im}{\cal T}^{\rm WE}
,
{\rm Im}{\cal T}^{\rm PI}
and
{\rm Im}{\cal T}^{\rm WA}
are given respectively by

\displaystyle{\rm Im}\,{\cal T}^{\rm WE}_{6}

\displaystyle=\frac{G_{F}^{2}}{12\pi}|V_{q_{1}b}|^{2}|V_{q_{3}q_{2}}|^{2}m_{b}^{2}\sqrt{\lambda(1,x_{1},x_{2})}\,\Bigg\{k_{1}\Big[\,\omega_{1}(x_{1},x_{2})\,{O}^{(q_{3})}_{1}-2\omega_{2}(x_{1},x_{2}){O}^{(q_{3})}_{2}\Big]

\displaystyle+k_{2}\Big[\omega_{1}(x_{1},x_{2})\tilde{O}^{(q_{3})}_{1}-2\omega_{2}(x_{1},x_{2})\tilde{O}^{(q_{3})}_{2}\Big]\Bigg\}\,,

(2.97)

\displaystyle{\rm Im}\,{\cal T}^{\rm PI}_{6}

\displaystyle=\frac{G_{F}^{2}}{2\pi}|V_{q_{1}b}|^{2}|V_{q_{3}q_{2}}|^{2}m_{b}^{2}\sqrt{\lambda(1,x_{1},x_{3})}\,(1-x_{1}-x_{3})\,\Big[k_{3}{O}^{(q_{2})}_{1}+k_{4}\tilde{O}^{(q_{2})}_{1}\Big]\,,

(2.98)

and

\displaystyle{\rm Im}\,{\cal T}^{\rm WA}_{6}

\displaystyle=\frac{G_{F}^{2}}{12\pi}|V_{q_{1}b}|^{2}|V_{q_{3}q_{2}}|^{2}m_{b}^{2}\sqrt{\lambda(1,x_{3},x_{2})}\,\Bigg\{k_{5}\Big[\,\omega_{1}(x_{3},x_{2})\,{O}^{(q_{1})}_{1}-2\omega_{2}(x_{3},x_{2}){O}^{(q_{1})}_{2}\Big]

\displaystyle+k_{6}\Big[\omega_{1}(x_{3},x_{2})\tilde{O}^{(q_{1})}_{1}-2\omega_{2}(x_{3},x_{2})\tilde{O}^{(q_{1})}_{2}\Big]\Bigg\}\,.

(2.99)

In Eqs. (
2.97
)-(
2.99
),
x_{i}
denotes the dimensionless mass parameters
x_{i}=m_{i}^{2}/m_{b}^{2}
, and the following basis for the dimension-six four-quark operators has been introduced

\displaystyle{O}^{(q)}_{1}=\Big(\bar{b}^{i}_{v}\gamma_{\nu}(1-\gamma_{5})q^{i}\Big)\Big(\bar{q}^{j}\gamma^{\nu}(1-\gamma_{5})b^{j}_{v}\Big)\,,

(2.100)

\displaystyle{O}^{(q)}_{2}=\Big(\bar{b}^{i}_{v}(1-\gamma_{5})q^{i}\Big)\Big(\bar{q}^{j}(1+\gamma_{5})b^{j}_{v}\Big)\,,

(2.101)

\displaystyle\tilde{O}^{(q)}_{1}=\Big(\bar{b}^{i}_{v}\gamma_{\nu}(1-\gamma_{5})t^{a}_{ij}q^{j}\Big)\Big(\bar{q}^{l}\gamma^{\nu}(1-\gamma_{5})t^{a}_{lm}b^{m}_{v}\Big)\,,

(2.102)

\displaystyle\tilde{O}^{(q)}_{2}=\Big(\bar{b}^{i}_{v}(1-\gamma_{5})t^{a}_{ij}q^{j}\Big)\Big(\bar{q}^{l}(1+\gamma_{5})t^{a}_{lm}b^{m}_{v}\Big)\,.

(2.103)

2.2.2 Computation of Im
{\cal T}^{(4q)}_{7}

By including
1/m_{b}
corrections to the incoming four-momentum
p^{\mu}
as well as to the heavy quark field
b(x)
, in Eqs. (
2.80
), (
2.88
), and (
2.94
), leads to dimension-seven contributions to the WE, PI, and WA topologies. In the following, we discuss in detail only the first two cases since the corresponding expression for WA can be immediately obtained using Eq. (
2.93
). However,
before considering the specific contributions separately, it is convenient to derive some general results which will facilitate the computation.
We recall that the incoming momentum
p^{\mu}
is the sum (for WE and WA), or the difference (for PI), of the
b
-quark and of the light-quark momentum, i.e.
p^{\mu}=p_{b}^{\mu}\pm p_{q}^{\mu}
, here
p^{\mu}_{b}=m_{b}v^{\mu}+k^{\mu}
, while
q=q_{1},q_{2},q_{3}
, respectively for the case of WA, PI and WE. Taking into account that
k\sim p_{q}\ll m_{b}
, the square of the incoming momentum can be written, up to terms of order
{\cal O}(1/m_{b}^{2})
, as
p^{2}=m_{b}^{2}\,(1+z)
, with the small parameter
z
given by

z=2\,\frac{v\cdot k}{m_{b}}\pm\,2\,\frac{v\cdot p_{q}}{m_{b}}\ll 1\,.

(2.104)

Correspondingly, the heavy quark field
b(x)
, using the framework of the HQET, see Section
1.2
, can be expanded in powers of
1/(2m_{b})
, as

b(x)=e^{-im_{b}v\cdot x}\left(h_{v}(x)+\frac{i\not{D}_{\perp}}{2m_{b}}h_{v}(x)+{\cal O}\left(\frac{1}{m_{b}^{2}}\right)\right)\,,

(2.105)

where
h_{v}(x)
denotes the effective heavy quark field, which coincides with
b_{v}(x)
at leading order in
1/m_{b}
, and which obeys to the equation of motion
(iv\cdot D)h_{v}(x)=0
, following from the HQET Lagrangian in Eq. (
1.44
). However, in the original QCD Lagrangian in Eq. (
1.58
), there are also subleading contributions suppressed by the heavy quark mass, which are treated as perturbations to
{\cal L}_{HQET}
, cf.
{\cal L}_{power}
in Eq. (
1.59
), so that, in the expansion of the matrix element of an operator containing the heavy quark field, their effect must be included in a standard perturbative way by taking the time ordered product of
{\cal L}_{power}
with the corresponding leading order operators, see Eq. (
1.64
). It then follows that

\displaystyle\bar{b}\Gamma_{\mu}q\bar{q}\Gamma_{\nu}b

\displaystyle\simeq\bar{h}_{v}\Gamma_{\mu}q\bar{q}\Gamma_{\nu}h_{v}+\frac{1}{2m_{b}}\bigg[\bar{h}_{v}\Gamma_{\mu}q\bar{q}\Gamma_{\nu}i\not{D}h_{v}+\bar{h}_{v}(-i\overset{\leftarrow}{\not{D}})\Gamma_{\mu}q\bar{q}\Gamma_{\nu}h_{v}\bigg]

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{m_{b}}i\int d^{4}y\,{\rm T}\Big\{\bar{h}_{v}\Gamma_{\mu}q\bar{q}\Gamma_{\nu}h_{v},{\cal L}_{1}(y)\Big\}+{\cal O}\left(\frac{1}{m_{b}^{2}}\right)\,,

(2.106)

and for brevity we have not specified the colour structure.
Note that in Eq. (
2.106
), the equation of motion for
h_{v}(x)
has been used to replace the action of
D_{\perp}^{\mu}
with that of
D_{\mu}
and that the symbol
\simeq
refers to the fact that left and right hand side of the equation must be evaluated respectively between QCD and HQET states, cf. Eq. (
1.64
) and see for more details Ref.
[
109
]

15
15

15

However, for simplicity, in the following we will just use an equal sign.
.
Moreover,
the non local operator in the second line of Eq. (
2.106
), parametrises the contribution of the first power correction to the QCD Lagrangian, i.e.
{\cal L}_{1}
in Eq. (
1.65
).

Taking into account that the action of a derivative on
h_{v}(x)
, returns only small frequencies of the order of
k
and that
q(x)
describes an outgoing antiquark with momentum
p_{q}^{\mu}
, we respectively have
16
16

16

Recall that in the FS gauge
A_{\mu}(0)=0
, and that the action of a partial derivative at the origin can be replaced with that of a covariant derivative, see Section
1.3
and also Appendix
A
.

v\cdot k\,\Big(\bar{h}_{v}\Gamma_{\mu}q\bar{q}\Gamma_{\nu}h_{v}\Big)=\lim_{x\to 0}\Big(\bar{h}_{v}(x)\Gamma_{\mu}q(x)\bar{q}(x)\Gamma_{\nu}iv\cdot Dh_{v}(x)\Big)=0\,,

(2.107)

while

v\cdot p_{q}\,\Big(\bar{h}_{v}\Gamma_{\mu}q\bar{q}\Gamma_{\nu}h_{v}\Big)=\lim_{x\to 0}\Big(\bar{h}_{v}(x)\Gamma_{\mu}(-iv\cdot D)q(x)\bar{q}(x)\Gamma_{\nu}h_{v}(x)\Big)=-\bar{h}_{v}\Gamma_{\mu}iv\cdot Dq\bar{q}\Gamma_{\nu}h_{v}\,,

(2.108)

where Eq. (
2.107
) vanishes due to the equation of motion for
h_{v}
, and in Eq. (
2.108
), the small momentum
p_{q}
has resulted in a dimension-seven operator with a covariant derivative acting on
q
.
Consider now the expansion of the product of two momenta, namely

p^{\mu}p^{\nu}=m_{b}^{2}\,\left(v^{\mu}v^{\nu}+\frac{v^{\mu}k^{\nu}}{m_{b}}+\frac{v^{\nu}k^{\mu}}{m_{b}}+\frac{v^{\mu}p_{q}^{\nu}}{m_{b}}+\frac{v^{\nu}p_{q}^{\mu}}{m_{b}}+{\cal O}\left(\frac{1}{m_{b}^{2}}\right)\right)\,,

(2.109)

which, combined with the result in Eq. (
2.106
), yields

\displaystyle\hskip 184.9429ptp^{\mu}p^{\nu}\Big(\bar{b}\Gamma_{\mu}q\bar{q}\Gamma_{\nu}b\Big)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt=m_{b}^{2}\,\Bigg(\bar{h}_{v}(1-\gamma_{5})q\bar{q}(1+\gamma_{5})h_{v}+\frac{1}{m_{b}}\bar{h}_{v}(1-\gamma_{5})q\bar{q}(1+\gamma_{5})i\not{D}h_{v}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{m_{b}}i\int d^{4}y{\rm T}\Big\{\bar{h}_{v}(1-\gamma_{5})q\bar{q}(1+\gamma_{5})h_{v},{\cal L}_{1}(y)\Big\}-2\frac{m_{q}}{m_{b}}\bar{h}_{v}(1-\gamma_{5})q\bar{q}(1-\gamma_{5})h_{v}+\ldots\Bigg)\,,

(2.110)

where the ellipsis stand for terms of order
{\cal O}(1/m_{b}^{2})
. Notice that in Eq. (
2.110
), the anti-commutation relation
\not{v}\not{D}=-\not{D}\not{v}+2v\cdot D
,
and the corresponding equations of motion for
h_{v}(x)
and for the light field, i.e.
(i\not{D}-m_{q})q(x)=0
,
have been used. Moreover, we have taken into account that operators related by Hermitian conjugation lead to the same matrix element, hence a factor of 2 has been included. Specifically these are

\bar{h}_{v}(-i\overset{\leftarrow}{\not{D}})(1-\gamma_{5})\bar{q}(1+\gamma_{5})h_{v}=\Big[\bar{h}_{v}(1-\gamma_{5})q\bar{q}(1+\gamma_{5})(i\not{D})h_{v}\Big]^{\dagger}\,,

(2.111)

and

\bar{h}_{v}(1+\gamma_{5})q\bar{q}(1+\gamma_{5})h_{v}=\Big[\bar{h}_{v}(1-\gamma_{5})q\bar{q}(1-\gamma_{5})h_{v}\Big]^{\dagger}\,.

(2.112)

Finally, we introduce the dimension-seven four-quark operator basis, which for clarity, we split into three categories, namely

\displaystyle{\cal P}^{(q)}_{1}

\displaystyle=m_{q}\,\big(\bar{h}_{v}^{i}(1-\gamma_{5})q^{i}\big)\big(\bar{q}^{j}(1-\gamma_{5})h^{j}_{v}\big)\,,

(2.113)

\displaystyle{\cal P}^{(q)}_{2}

\displaystyle=\big(\bar{h}^{i}_{v}\gamma_{\mu}(1-\gamma_{5})iv\cdot Dq^{i}\big)\big(\bar{q}^{j}\gamma^{\mu}(1-\gamma_{5})h^{j}_{v}\big)\,,

(2.114)

\displaystyle{\cal P}^{(q)}_{3}

\displaystyle=\big(\bar{h}^{i}_{v}(1-\gamma_{5})iv\cdot Dq^{i}\big)\big(\bar{q}^{j}(1+\gamma_{5})h^{j}_{v}\big)\,,

(2.115)

parametrising the effect of the light-quark momentum
p_{q}
,

\displaystyle{\cal R}^{(q)}_{1}

\displaystyle=\big(\bar{h}^{i}_{v}\gamma_{\mu}(1-\gamma_{5})q^{i}\big)\big(\bar{q}^{j}\gamma^{\mu}(1-\gamma_{5})i\not{D}h_{v}^{j}\big)\,,

(2.116)

\displaystyle{\cal R}^{(q)}_{2}

\displaystyle=\big(\bar{h}^{i}_{v}(1-\gamma_{5})q^{i}\big)\big(\bar{q}^{j}(1+\gamma_{5})i\not{D}h_{v}^{j}\big)\,,

(2.117)

describing local contributions due to the expansion of the heavy-quark field,
and

\displaystyle{\cal M}_{1}^{(q)}

\displaystyle=i\int d^{4}y{\rm T}\Big\{{\cal O}^{(q)}_{1},{\cal O}_{I}(y)\Big\}\,,

(2.118)

\displaystyle{\cal M}_{2}^{(q)}

\displaystyle=i\int d^{4}y{\rm T}\Big\{{\cal O}^{(q)}_{1},{\cal O}_{II}(y)\Big\}\,,

(2.119)

\displaystyle{\cal M}_{3}^{(q)}

\displaystyle=i\int d^{4}y{\rm T}\Big\{{\cal O}^{(q)}_{2},{\cal O}_{I}(y)\Big\}\,,

(2.120)

\displaystyle{\cal M}_{4}^{(q)}

\displaystyle=i\int d^{4}y{\rm T}\Big\{{\cal O}^{(q)}_{2},{\cal O}_{II}(y)\Big\}\,,

(2.121)

with
{\cal O}_{I,II}
defined in Eqs. (
1.69
), (
1.70
),
corresponding to the non-local contributions generated by taking the time ordered product of the
1/m_{b}
correction in the Lagrangian in Eq. (
1.59
), with the dimension-six local HQET operators

\displaystyle{\cal O}^{(q)}_{1}=\Big(\bar{h}^{i}_{v}\gamma_{\nu}(1-\gamma_{5})q^{i}\Big)\Big(\bar{q}^{j}\gamma^{\nu}(1-\gamma_{5})h^{j}_{v}\Big)\,,

(2.122)

\displaystyle{\cal O}^{(q)}_{2}=\Big(\bar{h}^{i}_{v}(1-\gamma_{5})q^{i}\Big)\Big(\bar{q}^{j}(1+\gamma_{5})h^{j}_{v}\Big)\,,

(2.123)

\displaystyle\tilde{\cal O}^{(q)}_{1}=\Big(\bar{h}^{i}_{v}\gamma_{\nu}(1-\gamma_{5})t^{a}_{ij}q^{j}\Big)\Big(\bar{q}^{l}\gamma^{\nu}(1-\gamma_{5})t^{a}_{lm}h^{m}_{v}\Big)\,,

(2.124)

\displaystyle\tilde{\cal O}^{(q)}_{2}=\Big(\bar{h}^{i}_{v}(1-\gamma_{5})t^{a}_{ij}q^{j}\Big)\Big(\bar{q}^{l}(1+\gamma_{5})t^{a}_{lm}h^{m}_{v}\Big)\,.

(2.125)

Note that the
1/m_{b}
contributions arising from the expansion of the heavy quark momentum vanish due to the equation of motion for
h_{v}(x)
.
Apart from the operators in Eqs. (
2.113
)-(
2.121
), the basis includes also the corresponding colour-octet operators
\tilde{\cal P}_{1}^{(q)},\tilde{\cal P}_{2}^{(q)},\tilde{\cal P}_{3}^{(q)},

\tilde{\cal R}_{1}^{(q)},\tilde{\cal R}_{2}^{(q)}
, containing respectively the colour matrices
t^{a}
, and
\tilde{\cal M}_{1}^{(q)},\tilde{\cal M}_{2}^{(q)},\tilde{\cal M}_{3}^{(q)}\tilde{\cal M}_{4}^{(q)}
, in which we must replace
{\cal O}_{i}\to\tilde{\cal O}_{i}
. For brevity however, we omit to explicitly show them.

With the above results, it is straightforward to obtain the expansion of Eq. (
2.80
), up to order
{\cal O}(1/m_{b}^{2})
, namely

\displaystyle{\rm Im}\,{\cal T}^{\rm WE}

\displaystyle=\frac{G_{F}^{2}}{12\pi}|V_{q_{1}b}|^{2}|V_{q_{3}q_{2}}|^{2}m_{b}^{2}\sqrt{\lambda(1,x_{1},x_{2})}\,\left(1+\frac{(1-x_{1}-x_{2})}{\lambda(1,x_{1},x_{2})}z\right)

\displaystyle\times\Bigg\{k_{1}\,\Bigg[\big(\omega_{1}(x_{1},x_{2})-y_{1}(x_{1},x_{2})\,z\big)\Big({\cal O}_{1}^{(q_{3})}+\frac{{\cal R}_{1}^{(q_{3})}}{m_{b}}+\frac{{\cal M}_{1}^{(q_{3})}}{m_{b}}+\frac{{\cal M}_{2}^{(q_{3})}}{m_{b}}\Big)

\displaystyle-2\,\big(\omega_{2}(x_{1},x_{2})-y_{2}(x_{1},x_{2})\,z\big)\left(1-z\right)\Big({\cal O}_{2}^{(q_{3})}+\frac{{\cal R}_{2}^{(q_{3})}}{m_{b}}+\frac{{\cal M}_{3}^{(q_{3})}}{m_{b}}+\frac{{\cal M}_{4}^{(q_{3})}}{m_{b}}-2\frac{{\cal P}_{1}^{(q_{3})}}{m_{b}}\Big)\Bigg]

\displaystyle+\Big(k_{1}\to k_{2},\,{\rm singlet}\to{\rm octet}\Big)+{\cal O}\left(\frac{1}{m_{b}^{2}}\right)\Bigg\}\,,

(2.126)

where

\displaystyle y_{1}(a,b)=2\,(a-b)^{2}+(a+b)\,,\qquad y_{2}(a,b)=4\,(a-b)^{2}-(a+b)\,,

(2.127)

and the small parameter
z
is defined in Eq. (
2.104
) (with the plus sign).
The leading order result in Eq. (
2.126
) reproduces the dimension-six expression obtained in Eq. (
2.97
), but with the QCD operators, i.e.
Q_{i}^{(q_{3})},\tilde{Q}_{i}^{(q_{3})}
, see Eqs. (
2.100
)-(
2.103
), replaced by the corresponding HQET ones i.e.
{\cal O}_{i}^{(q_{3})},\tilde{\cal O}_{i}^{(q_{3})}
, see Eqs. (
2.122
)-(
2.125
). Moreover, using Eqs. (
2.107
), (
2.108
), to rewrite
z
in terms of derivatives acting on light-quark field, we arrive at the final dimension-seven contribution, which reads

\displaystyle{\rm Im}\,{\cal T}^{\rm WE}_{7}

\displaystyle=\frac{G_{F}^{2}}{12\pi}|V_{q_{1}b}|^{2}|V_{q_{3}q_{2}}|^{2}m_{b}\sqrt{\lambda(1,x_{1},x_{2})}\,\Bigg\{k_{1}\Bigg[\omega_{1}(x_{1},x_{2})\Big({\cal R}_{1}^{(q_{3})}+{\cal M}_{1}^{(q_{3})}+{\cal M}_{2}^{(q_{3})}\Big)

\displaystyle+2\,\frac{(1-x_{1}-x_{2})}{\lambda(1,x_{1},x_{2})}\,\Big(2\,\omega_{2}(x_{1},x_{2})\,{\cal P}_{3}^{(q_{3})}-\omega_{1}(x_{1},x_{2})\,{\cal P}_{2}^{(q_{3})}\Big)

\displaystyle+2\,\omega_{2}(x_{1},x_{2})\Bigg(2\,\Big({\cal P}_{1}^{(q_{3})}-{\cal P}_{3}^{(q_{3})}\Big)-\Big({\cal R}_{2}^{(q_{3})}+{\cal M}_{3}^{(q_{3})}+{\cal M}_{4}^{(q_{3})}\Big)\Bigg)

\displaystyle+2\,\Big(y_{1}(x_{1},x_{2}){\cal P}_{2}^{(q_{3})}-2\,y_{2}(x_{1},x_{2})\,{\cal P}_{3}^{(q_{3})}\Big)\Bigg]

\displaystyle+\Big(k_{1}\to k_{2},\,{\rm singlet}\to{\rm octet}\Big)\Bigg\}\,.

(2.128)

Similarly,
it follows that the expansion of Eq. (
2.88
) up order
{\cal O}\left(1/m_{b}^{2}\right)
, yields

\displaystyle{\rm Im}\,{\cal T}^{\rm PI}

\displaystyle=\frac{G_{F}^{2}}{2\pi}|V_{q_{1}b}|^{2}|V_{q_{3}q_{2}}|^{2}m_{b}^{2}\sqrt{\lambda(1,x_{1},x_{3})}\,(1-x_{1}-x_{3})

\displaystyle\times\Bigg[1+\Bigg(\frac{(x_{1}+x_{3})}{(1-x_{1}-x_{3})}+\frac{(1-x_{1}-x_{3})}{\lambda(1,x_{1},x_{3})}\Bigg)\,z\Bigg]

\displaystyle\times\Bigg\{k_{3}\,\Big[{\cal O}_{1}^{(q_{2})}+\frac{{\cal R}_{1}^{(q_{2})}}{m_{b}}+\frac{{\cal M}_{1}^{(q_{2})}}{m_{b}}+\frac{{\cal M}_{2}^{(q_{2})}}{m_{b}}\Big]

\displaystyle+\Big(k_{3}\to k_{4}\,,\,{\rm singlet}\to{\rm octet}\Big)+{\cal O}\left(\frac{1}{m_{b}^{2}}\right)\Bigg\}\,,

(2.129)

with the small parameter
z
defined in Eq. (
2.104
) (with the minus sign).
Again, the leading order contribution in Eq. (
2.129
) reproduces to the dimension-six expression obtained in Eq. (
2.98
), but with the QCD operators, i.e.
Q_{i}^{(q_{2})},\tilde{Q}_{i}^{(q_{2})}
, see Eqs. (
2.100
)-(
2.103
) replaced by the corresponding HQET ones i.e.
{\cal O}_{i}^{(q_{2})},\tilde{\cal O}_{i}^{(q_{2})}
, see Eqs. (
2.122
)-(
2.125
). Moreover, using Eqs. (
2.107
), (
2.108
), to rewrite
z
in terms of derivatives acting on light-quark field we arrive at final dimension-seven result, which reads

\displaystyle{\rm Im}\,{\cal T}^{\rm PI}_{7}

\displaystyle=\frac{G_{F}^{2}}{2\pi}|V_{q_{1}b}|^{2}|V_{q_{3}q_{2}}|^{2}m_{b}\sqrt{\lambda(1,x_{1},x_{3})}\,(1-x_{1}-x_{3})\,

\displaystyle\times\Bigg\{k_{3}\,\Bigg[{\cal R}_{1}^{(q_{2})}+{\cal M}_{1}^{(q_{2})}+{\cal M}_{2}^{(q_{2})}+2\,\Bigg(\frac{(x_{1}+x_{3})}{(1-x_{1}-x_{3})}+\frac{(1-x_{1}-x_{3})}{\lambda(1,x_{1},x_{3})}\Bigg)\,{\cal P}^{(q_{2})}_{2}\Bigg]

\displaystyle+\,\Bigg(k_{3}\to k_{4},\,{\rm singlet}\to{\rm octet}\Bigg)\Bigg\}\,.

(2.130)

Finally, the corresponding dimension-seven contribution to the WA topology is simply obtained by replacing
C_{1}\leftrightarrow C_{2}
, and
q_{1}\leftrightarrow q_{3}
, into Eq. (
2.128
), namely

\displaystyle{\rm Im}\,{\cal T}^{\rm WA}_{7}

\displaystyle=\frac{G_{F}^{2}}{12\pi}|V_{q_{1}b}|^{2}|V_{q_{3}q_{2}}|^{2}m_{b}\sqrt{\lambda(1,x_{3},x_{2})}\,\Bigg\{k_{5}\Bigg[\omega_{1}(x_{3},x_{2})\Big({\cal R}_{1}^{(q_{1})}+{\cal M}_{1}^{(q_{1})}+{\cal M}_{2}^{(q_{1})}\Big)

\displaystyle+2\,\frac{(1-x_{3}-x_{2})}{\lambda(1,x_{3},x_{2})}\,\Big(2\,\omega_{2}(x_{3},x_{2})\,{\cal P}_{3}^{(q_{1})}-\omega_{1}(x_{3},x_{2})\,{\cal P}_{2}^{(q_{1})}\Big)

\displaystyle+2\,\omega_{2}(x_{3},x_{2})\Bigg(2\,\Big({\cal P}_{1}^{(q_{1})}-{\cal P}_{3}^{(q_{1})}\Big)-\Big({\cal R}_{2}^{(q_{1})}+{\cal M}_{3}^{(q_{1})}+{\cal M}_{4}^{(q_{1})}\Big)\Bigg)

\displaystyle+2\,\Big(y_{1}(x_{3},x_{2}){\cal P}_{2}^{(q_{1})}-2\,y_{2}(x_{3},x_{2})\,{\cal P}_{3}^{(q_{1})}\Big)\Bigg]

\displaystyle+\Big(k_{5}\to k_{6},\,{\rm singlet}\to{\rm octet}\Big)\Bigg\}\,.

(2.131)

We emphasise that the results in Eqs. (
2.128
), (
2.130
), and (
2.131
), can be applied without any difference, to the description of baryons as well. In fact, in this case, the sign change in front of the light-quark momentum i.e.
p^{\mu}=p_{b}^{\mu}\mp p_{q}^{\mu}
, is compensated by the same sign change in front of the operators
{\cal P}_{i}^{(q)}
,
i=1,2,3
, since now
q(x)
describes an incoming quark with momentum
p_{q}^{\mu}
.

To conclude, we note that we have derived the dimension-seven contribution in terms of operators defined in HQET, the corresponding expressions in terms of QCD fields are obtained by expanding Eqs. (
2.80
), (
2.88
), and (
2.94
), only in the small momentum
p_{q}
of the light spectator quark, but not in the HQET field. This results in the following basis

\displaystyle{P}^{(q)}_{1}

\displaystyle=m_{q}\,\big(\bar{b}^{i}(1-\gamma_{5})q^{i}\big)\big(\bar{q}^{j}(1-\gamma_{5})b^{j}\big)\,,

(2.132)

\displaystyle{P}^{(q)}_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{m_{b}}\big(\bar{b}^{i}\overset{\leftarrow}{D_{\nu}}\gamma_{\mu}(1-\gamma_{5})D^{\nu}q^{i}\big)\big(\bar{q}^{j}\gamma^{\mu}(1-\gamma_{5})b^{j}\big)\,,

(2.133)

\displaystyle{P}^{(q)}_{3}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{m_{b}}\big(\bar{b}^{i}\overset{\leftarrow}{D_{\nu}}(1-\gamma_{5})D^{\nu}q^{i}\big)\big(\bar{q}^{j}(1+\gamma_{5})b^{j}\big)\,,

(2.134)

together with the colour-octet operators
\tilde{P}^{(q)}_{i}
. Due to the presence, in Eqs. (
2.133
), (
2.134
), of a covariant derivative acting on the
b
-quark field, which scales as
m_{b}
at this order, there is no explicit power counting, differently to the HQET basis. The corresponding QCD result for Eqs. (
2.128
), (
2.130
), (
2.131
), can be immediately obtained by setting in these expressions,

{\cal R}^{(q)}_{i},{\cal R}^{(q)}_{i},{\cal M}^{(q)}_{i},{\cal M}^{(q)}_{i}
to zero and by replacing
{\cal P}^{(q)}_{i}\to P^{(q)}_{i}
and
\tilde{\cal P}^{(q)}_{i}\to\tilde{P}^{(q)}_{i}
. We stress that, in this case, the difference in the operator basis is compensated by the different parametrisation of the corresponding matrix elements in QCD and in HQET, cf. Chapter
4
and Appendix
G
.

Chapter 3   Contribution of the Darwin Operator

In Section
2.1
we have discussed the calculation of the dimension-three and dimension-five contributions to the HQE of a
B
meson due to the single mode
b\to c\bar{u}d
and using the representation of the quark-propagator in coordinate space given in Eqs. (
1.121
), (
1.124
). In the present chapter, we generalise the above results by computing the coefficients of the two-quark operators in the HQE, for the generic
b\to q_{1}\bar{q}_{2}q_{3}
transition and up to terms of order
1/m_{b}^{3}
, where the latter describe the contribution of the Darwin operator, see Eq. (
1.157
)
1
1

1

We stress that again, the computation is performed only at LO-QCD.
. The representation derived in Ref.
[
130
]
for the calculation of the imaginary part of integrals containing Bessel functions, is not sufficient for the case in which more than one propagator is massive, hence now the whole computation is performed in momentum space, using the expression of the quark-propagator given in Eqs. (
1.98
), (
1.102
). Specifically, we start in Section
3.1
, by computing the expansion of
{\rm Im}{\cal T}^{(2q)}
up to order
1/m_{b}^{3}
. We will find that the coefficients of the Darwin operator develop IR divergences in correspondence of the emission of a soft gluon from a light quark propagator. In particular, the singularities originate from the expansion of the propagator of the up, down, and strange quarks, which we consider massless, and are logarithmic, namely the corresponding coefficients have the asymptotic form
\sim\log(m_{q}^{2}/m_{b}^{2})
in the limit
m_{q}\to 0
, with
q=u,d,s
. As described e.g. in Ref.
[
73
]
, these logarithmic infrared divergences are due to the mixing between operators of the same dimension under renormalisation. This is discussed in Section
3.2
, where we compute the one-loop diagram describing the mixing of the four-quark and the Darwin operators and perform their renormalisation in order to ensure the cancellation of the IR divergences. Finally, the complete expressions for the coefficients of the Darwin operator are presented in Section
3.3
together with a discussion of the results. The structure closely follows the one of Ref.
[
137
]
.

3.1 Computation of
\Gamma^{(2q)}(B)
up to order
1/m_{b}^{3}

The starting point for the computation of the contribution of two-quark operators is Eq. (
2.10
), which we state here again for practicality, i.e.
2
2

2

Note that the superscript
(q_{1}\bar{q}_{2}q_{3})
is often omitted for the sake of a cleaner notation, however it must be always understood.

\displaystyle{\cal T}^{(2q)}=C_{1}^{2}\,{\cal T}^{(2q)}_{11}+\,2\,C_{1}C_{2}\,\left(\frac{1}{N_{c}}{\cal T}^{(2q)}_{11}+2\,{\cal T}^{(2q)}_{13}\right)+C_{2}^{2}\,{\cal T}^{(2q)}_{22}\,,

(3.1)

with Eqs. (
2.12
), (
2.13
), compactly written as

\displaystyle{\cal T}_{11\{13\}}^{(2q)}

\displaystyle=-\frac{G_{F}^{2}}{2}|V_{q_{1}b}|^{2}|V_{q_{3}q_{2}}|^{2}\,i\int d^{4}x\,\,\bar{b}\,\gamma_{\nu}(1-\gamma_{5})\{t^{a}\}iS^{(q_{1})}(0,x)\gamma_{\mu}(1-\gamma_{5})b(x)

\displaystyle\times{\rm Tr}\Biggl[\gamma^{\nu}(1-\gamma_{5})\{t^{a}\}iS^{(q_{3})}(0,x)\gamma^{\mu}(1-\gamma_{5})iS^{(q_{2})}(x,0)\Biggr]+(x\leftrightarrow 0)\,.

(3.2)

Note that only in the case of
{\cal T}_{13}^{(2q)}
the
SU(3)_{c}
generators
t^{a}
appear on the r.h.s. of Eq. (
3.2
), as indicated by the curly brackets, and that a summation over colour indices is understood. Furthermore, the corresponding expression for
{\cal T}_{22}^{(2q)}
has been omitted, since it can be derived from that of
{\cal T}_{11}^{(2q)}
by taking into account the result in Eq. (
2.18
).

Being interested in the expansion of
{\cal T}^{(2q)}
up to order
1/m_{b}^{3}
, we must now substitute in Eq. (
3.2
), the complete expression of the quark propagator derived in Section
1.3
, namely including also terms proportional to one covariant derivative of the gluon field strength tensor
D_{\rho}G_{\mu\nu}
. It is worth remarking that the colour structure of Eq. (
3.2
) allows for a straightforward treatment of colour, in complete analogy to what has already been discussed in Section
2.1.2
. To this end, it is convenient to single out the colour structure of the propagator in Eq. (
1.97
), which schematically reads

{\cal S}_{jk}^{(q_{i})}(p)={\cal S}_{0}^{(q_{i})}(p)\,\delta_{jk}+{\cal S}_{1}^{(q_{i})\,a}(p)\,t^{a}_{jk}+{\cal O}\big(t^{a}t^{b}\big)\,,\qquad i=1,2,3\,,

(3.3)

where
{\cal S}_{0}^{(q_{i})}(p)
denotes the Fourier transform of the free-quark propagator given in Eq. (
1.74
), while
{\cal S}_{1}^{(q_{i})}(p)
includes higher order corrections due to operators of dimension-two and dimension-three describing the emission of one soft gluon field, see Eq. (
1.98
), and again the superscript
(q_{i})
has been introduced in order to distinguish between different quarks. As consequence of fact that
{\rm Tr}[t^{a}]=0
and that terms quadratic in the gluon field strength tensor correspond to operators in the HQE of at least dimension-seven, a soft gluon cannot be emitted from all the propagators in Eq. (
3.2
). Specifically, in the case of
{\cal T}_{11}^{(2q)}
only the contribution of
S_{1}^{(q_{1})}
is non zero and hence, because of Eq. (
2.18
), only
S_{1}^{(q_{3})}
contributes to
{\cal T}_{22}^{(2q)}
, see Figure
3.1
. Finally, in the case of
{\cal T}_{13}^{(2q)}
, we can independently expand the two propagators inside the trace and both
S_{1}^{(q_{2})}
and
S_{1}^{(q_{3})}
give a non vanishing contribution, see Figure
3.2
.

Figure 3.1:
Two-loop diagrams describing power corrections due to the expansion of the quark propagator up to order
1/m_{b}^{3}
, from the
Q_{1}\otimes Q_{1}
(left) and
Q_{2}\otimes Q_{2}
(right) contributions.

Figure 3.2:
Two-loop diagrams describing power corrections due to the expansion of the quark propagator up to order
1/m_{b}^{3}
, from the
Q_{1}\otimes Q_{3}
contribution.

Substituting Eqs. (
1.96
), (
1.100
) into Eq. (
3.2
), and taking into account Eq. (
2.18
), we respectively obtain

3

Note that the replacement in Eq. (
